## M1-21 Transport protection

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-21/1 | 2026-10-12 | A `secret_ref` key with an empty default (chapter cell "leer": `server.tls_cert_ref`, `server.tls_key_ref`) is optional: the empty text, as default or from a source, is the value "no reference" and switches its feature off; `bothy config check --secrets` prints `not set` for it and reads nothing. A `secret_ref` key with a reference as default still refuses the empty text. This replaces the rule "no empty `secret_ref` default" of M1-07/11, as that ruling foresaw. | 16 "5.2 Secret-Provider" names "leer" for secrets of a feature an operator can switch off; a separate flag per feature would be a second switch for the same fact. |
| M1-21/2 | 2026-10-12 | The cross-checks `tls_pair` (both TLS references or neither), `tls_https` (TLS needs an `https` `server.public_url`) and `hsts_whole_seconds` (`http.hsts_max_age` a multiple of 1s; `Min` `0s` refuses negative values) live in `internal/wire` with the keys, so their tests are in `internal/wire` and not in `internal/platform/config`, which has no key of its own. `httpserver.ChainConfig` checks `http.hsts_max_age` once more for callers that bypass the loader. | The keys and the cross-checks of the server are registered in `internal/wire`; a kind rule "whole seconds" for one key would add a field to `config.Key` for a single user. |
| M1-21/3 | 2026-10-12 | `httpserver` may use `platform/secrets` (module register): `TransportConfig` holds `secrets.Ref`, and the provider comes in through the narrow interface `httpserver.SecretGetter` (`Get`), which `*secrets.Provider` satisfies. `platform/secrets` stays unchanged. | The brief fixes the reference type of `platform/secrets`; that package has no provider interface, and adding one there is outside the scope of this ticket. |
| M1-21/4 | 2026-10-12 | The TLS material is checked at start step 2 by `httpserver.CheckTransport` and loaded again by `ListenPublic` at step 8 (the provider cache makes the second read free within `secrets.cache_ttl`). Only `api` and `all` check it; the worker opens no public listener and never reads the TLS references. | Bad material must end the start before the database wait (02 "15.1 Start"); a worker container need not mount the certificate. |
| M1-21/5 | 2026-10-12 | A rotated pair goes through the same checks as at start (PEM chain, matching key, validity, host of `server.public_url`). A failed pair of versions keeps the previous certificate and logs `TLS certificate rotation failed, keeping the previous certificate` (`error.type` `tls_rotation`) once; it is parsed again at most once per minute, so a pair refused for a reason that passes with time (a leaf not yet valid for the server clock) is picked up without a restart. A good pair clears the memory of failed pairs. `GetCertificate` holds its lock over read and swap, so an older pair cannot replace a newer one. A rotation that replaces the two files at different times can log one such line until both are read again. `TransportConfig.Clock` (nil: system clock) drives validity and retry. | 02 "14.4": a failed rotation keeps the old value; one line per version, not per handshake, and at most one parse per minute for a broken file. |
| M1-21/6 | 2026-10-12 | The refusal log has constant messages and names the listener in `bothy.reason` (value `public`, from the fixed list of listener names); the first refusal per listener has the hint to add the proxy to `server.trusted_proxies` (Warn), later ones only `plaintext connection from an untrusted peer refused` (Debug). | The log allowlist of `platform/otel` keeps only catalog keys and the catalog has no listener key; `sloglint` requires constant messages. |
| M1-21/7 | 2026-10-12 | SEC-HDR-1 (with HSTS) is set in the outermost step before the next handler runs, and a secure writer applies it once more right before the header block goes out, so no handler can drop `nosniff`, weaken HSTS or send `Server`. The internal listener uses the same writer through `InternalHandler`, which also sets `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` (health answers JSON) and answers unknown paths and methods with problem bodies; health registers `GET` patterns, which also match `HEAD`. | Design note 7 asks that inner responses inherit the headers; applying them at write time closes the gap of a handler that changes them. |
| M1-21/8 | 2026-10-12 | The TLS listener is an own wrapper around the TCP listener with `tls.Server` per connection and `NextProtos` `h2`, `http/1.1`, the list `net/http` uses for `ServeTLS`; `http.Server.Serve` then serves HTTP/2 on connections that negotiated it. | The brief has `ListenPublic` return the listener; the wrapper keeps the TLS configuration inspectable for SEC-T17 (no own cipher list). |
| M1-21/9 | 2026-10-12 | The SEC-T03 row "503 while shutting down" on the public listener is the `503 unavailable` problem of the shared writer: the public listener has no own shutdown answer (readiness turns false, the drain waits, then `Shutdown` closes the listener). The shutdown state on the internal listener is `/readyz` `503` with `shutting_down`, covered by the row "readyz starting" (same writer) and by `TestServeReadyzDuringDrain`. | Nothing in M1 answers 503 because of the shutdown on the public listener; a test row for a path that does not exist would prove nothing. |
| M1-21/10 | 2026-10-12 | Not covered by SEC-HDR-1: responses that `net/http` writes itself outside any handler (`400` for a malformed request line, `431` for too large headers, the plain answer to plaintext on the TLS port). The `429` row of SEC-T03 waits for M1-19 (`internal/platform/ratelimit` is not on the base). | Those answers are written by the standard library before the handler chain exists; they carry no body of the API. |
| M1-21/11 | 2026-10-12 | Work happens on branch `feat/M1-21-transport-protection` from `int/m1-wave4` (`76b79a5`); the branch is not merged into the base locally and the worktree stays, because the launcher's PR lane ports the result. This replaces the merge step of the brief's definition of done. | Instruction of the launcher, which takes precedence over the brief. |

## M1-24 Audit chain, recorder and aggregation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-24/1 | 2026-10-12 | Canonical form of an IP address for `ip_hmac`: the client IP of `kernel/reqmeta` after `netip.Addr.Unmap()` and without zone, in the text form of `netip.Addr.String()` (IPv4 dotted decimal, IPv6 compressed lower case per RFC 5952); `ip_hmac = hex(HMAC-SHA-256(key, text))` in full length (64 characters). Every entry of a request that carries a client IP gets `ip_hmac` and `ip_key_id`, also for an authenticated actor. | One text per address, so `::ffff:192.0.2.44` and `192.0.2.44` and an address with zone give one pseudonym (`TestIPHMAC_MappedEqualsIPv4`, `TestIPHMAC_ZoneIgnored`); the full length keeps the owner's check of a known address unambiguous within the retention (20 "4.5"). |
| M1-24/2 | 2026-10-12 | The closed reason list of 07 "4.1" lives in `internal/kernel/auditing` (`Reason`, one constant per row in table order, `KnownReasons`, `Reason.Known`); `authz` reuses it with M1-28. `TestReasonsMatchChapter07` parses the table and compares both ways and the order. | R-DM-28: one list, held closed by a test; the kernel port is the only package both `audit` and `authz` may import. |
| M1-24/3 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave4` at `76b79a5`, branch `feat/M1-24-audit-chain` in an own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in `internal/audit/README.md` (the brief asks for German); its German headings stay, because the module README layout is shared by all modules. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language. |
| M1-24/4 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/txn` gets `Detach(ctx)`: a copy of ctx outside the unit of work with every other value. `RecordDetached`, the aggregator and the keyring run `UnitOfWork.Do` on it and so begin an own transaction even when the caller is inside one. The per-unit state the hook needs (`Key`, `Load`, `Store`) already existed. | The brief requires an own unit that never joins an ambient one; `platform/pg` joins whenever `txn.InUnit(ctx)` holds, and hiding the unit is the smallest change that keeps actor, correlation ID and client IP. |
| M1-24/5 | 2026-10-12 | `Attrs` refuses `ip_hmac`, `ip_key_id` and `aggregate` (the recorder sets them); `reason` takes only `auditing.ReasonText` and `violations` only `auditing.Violations`, and these two values are refused under any other key. `ReasonText` is checked for emptiness and with `kernel/textcheck`; its length bound `audit.reason_max_chars` stays with the request validation of the action, which answers `400 too_long` (SEC-AUD-5), so the key is not in the schema yet. Codes and attribute keys are at most 64 bytes (`domain.MaxCodeLen`). | The brief's comment on `Attrs` names `violations` as reserved while its test list accepts `violations`; typed values under their reserved key satisfy both. A recorder that truncated or refused a long reason after the change would lose the entry or fail a valid action. |
| M1-24/6 | 2026-10-12 | The flush of the aggregation is the stop step `audit_denials` in `lifecycle.PhaseDatabase`, registered by the start step `audit` after `register` added the step `pool`; steps of one phase run in reverse registration order, so it runs before the pool closes in every role. `TestAudit_StartFlushesBeforeThePoolCloses` holds the order. No new lifecycle phase. | `lifecycle` has no audit phase and is outside the scope; the database phase is the last one that still has the pool. |
| M1-24/7 | 2026-10-12 | The module reports through the port `app.Reporter`, implemented by `adapters/logs` with constant messages and the keys `error.type`, `bothy.action`, `bothy.reason` and `db.response.status_code` (audit adapters may use pgx for the SQLSTATE). The count of dropped denials and the number of a missing row are not logged, and the principal of a dropped bucket is not logged either. | The app layer may not import `platform/otel/attr`; the attribute catalogue has no key for a count or a sequence number (adding one needs signals.md and the collector allowlist), and SEC-LOG-4 allows people in logs only as pseudonyms, which `platform/otel` derives and the app layer cannot reach. |
| M1-24/8 | 2026-10-12 | `Record` computes the IP pseudonym when it is called (work phase), not in the hook; the keyring reads and creates key rows only in its own transactions. A denial whose key is unavailable is aggregated without IP fields and reported; `Record` and `RecordDetached` fail instead. A correlation ID that is not a UUID is left out of the row. | A key row created in the caller's transaction would stay cached after a rollback while missing from the table; in the hook the second connection would be taken while holding `audit_seq`. `RecordDenial` must never return a storage error; losing the pseudonym is better than losing the denial. |
| M1-24/9 | 2026-10-12 | The queries of the brief get one more, `Rows` (rows `from_seq .. to_seq` in chain order, details decoded with `UseNumber`), and the hook inserts with one `INSERT ... SELECT` over parallel `unnest` arrays. | The chain tests recompute every hash from the row read back; M1-50 needs the same read. sqlc does not resolve the multi-argument `unnest` in `FROM`; set-returning functions in one select list run in lock step. |
| M1-24/10 | 2026-10-12 | After a failed write of aggregated denials the timer waits one `audit.deny_aggregate_window` before the next attempt; `Record` in the audit phase itself is an error. The rules of `privacy.audit_ip_key_period`, `privacy.audit_ip_key_retention` and `privacy.audit_ip_kek_ref` leave `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml`, because the keys entered the schema (rule D6). | A failed bucket is due at once again; retrying without a pause would spin while the database is down. An entry recorded after the hook ran would never be written. |
| M1-24/11 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (verdict JA, eight Minors). Fixed: the sqlc entry of `audit` writes `*.gen.go` files (M1-05/7); concurrent `Record` calls in one unit of work append under one lock, so no entry is lost (`TestRecord_ConcurrentInOneUnitKeepsEveryEntry`); a string attribute that parses as an IP address is refused although it matches the code pattern (K-DP-2); the final flush at shutdown (`Aggregator.Close`) reports every bucket it could not write with `DenialsDropped`. Not changed: a batch whose COMMIT succeeded but whose acknowledgement was lost is retried with the same row ids and fails on `audit_events_id_key` until the cap drops it (new ids would count the denials twice; the case needs a lost acknowledgement); the pool pressure of the keyring at a period change and the synchronous write of an evicted bucket are named in the README limits and follow from 20 "4.5" and SEC-AUD-6; the count in the drop log is M1-24/7. The identity entry of `sqlc.yaml` keeps its file names, because it is outside this ticket. | The owner rule lists Minors once; the four fixes were small and each has a test. |

Resource kinds match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`; `ResourceKind` constants cover the kinds of actions and events. | The catalogue holds this information; dropping it would force M1-28 to read `actions.yaml` again. No catalogue content is invented. |

Operation id, op name and module are not log attributes; they are in the returned `*app.RegistrationError`, one line per violation, which `bothy serve` writes to stderr. | The allowlist handler of `platform/otel` drops every key outside signals.md (INT-W2/7); adding keys to the catalogue of signals is outside this ticket. |

VerifyRegistrations also reports a registration without a module name. | One definition of "known" in production; the empty list documents where module tickets add their function. |
| M1-25/7 | 2026-10-12 | `.golangci.yml` excludes `misspell` for `internal/authz/domain/catalog_gen.go` (German 05 text such as `Autor`, like M1-17/27) and `gosec` G101 for `internal/kernel/authorize/catalog_ids_gen.go` (ids such as `auth.token.refresh` look like credentials). The coverage filter of `make coverage` matches `.gen.go` only, so the two `_gen.go` files count; the tests exercise every function in them. | Generated code cannot satisfy both linters; changing the Makefile coverage filter is outside the scope, and the files are covered. |
| M1-25/8 | 2026-10-12 | Text this ticket adds to German documents (02 section 4.5, `services/server/api/README.md`, the authz README) is English. | The launcher requires English for everything added; same precedent as M1-23/9 and M1-11/12. CONTRIBUTING asks for German specification text; the translation of the chapters is pending. |
| M1-25/9 | 2026-10-12 | Review findings fixed: catalogen reads exactly one YAML document, rejects null scalars and an empty resource list, type-checks the fields it does not store, writes both files through temporary files and renames them only when both are written, and refuses an identifier that equals a name declared by hand in the target package (read from the other Go files of the output directory) or one of the generated type and function names. The Semgrep rule also catches string literals passed to `Spec`, `Registry.Event`, `Registry.Op`, `Registry.ChannelOp` and a dot import; `TestNoConversionInsideThePackage` covers unqualified conversions inside `kernel/authorize`. The rule excludes only `*_gen.go`, `*.gen.go` and `*_test.go`. | Strict decoding and "fails with entry and field" also apply to these inputs; a generated file that does not compile is a worse message than the generator's. |

Minors fixed in the same change: explicit YAML tags, aliases, `null` and booleans in name lists and quoted `"true"` are rejected, YAML booleans count in any case; a failed second rename restores the first file; `EventSpec.Note` keeps the column "Hinweis"/"Zusatzbedingung"; the check runs before the HTTP chain is built; an unknown route action is logged with `bothy.action`; route messages name the pattern once; a route binding needs operation id, method and pattern; a zero or nil `Catalog` fails closed; tests for the telemetry adapter and the unreachable branches of `verifyMentioned`; repeated literals became constants (Sonar go:S1192); the in-package scan also finds parenthesised conversions. Not changed: a gateway or channel op registered by two modules stays accepted, and the Semgrep rule keeps the limits the authz README lists. | The brief asks the two-module rule only for event types, and a gateway op can legitimately be handled by one module per role later; type-aware checks of every implicit conversion are beyond Semgrep, and every such value still fails closed. |

This test file is outside the scope of the brief. | The full `make check` of this ticket failed once on it with both lines missing from the buffer although `/readyz` already answered 200: the child's stdout arrives through a pipe, so a line written before readiness can still be in flight. It passed 12 of 12 runs alone; the change removes the read-once race without changing what the test proves. |

## M1-14 Background jobs with River

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-14/1 | 2026-10-12 | The module of a job kind is the segment after the last `/internal/` of the argument type's package (`txn.ModuleOfPackage`); the tests use the stand-in module `internal/platform/jobs/internal/fakemodule`. `ValidateArgsType` also refuses unexported fields, which the brief does not list. | Same derivation as the txn registry (M1-13/1). An unexported field never reaches River's JSON, so the handler would see a zero value. |
| M1-14/2 | 2026-10-12 | `InsertTx` is allowed in the phases `rights_begin`, `work` and `rights_check`, and refuses arguments that do not survive River's JSON encoding (a zero `ids.ID` is no valid UUIDv7) with an internal error; `AddPeriodic` checks the same. | The brief forbids only `sync` and `audit`. Found in the first integration run: such arguments failed every attempt at run time; at the insert the caller's unit rolls back instead. |
| M1-14/3 | 2026-10-12 | A worker whose registry is empty does not start River; `Start` is a no-op then, as for `api`. | River v0.49.0 refuses to start a client without workers, and until M1-16 registers the first handler there is nothing to run. |
| M1-14/4 | 2026-10-12 | `internal/wire` registers two stop steps in phase `jobs`: the soft stop with budget `shutdown.jobs_soft_timeout` (counted in the budget sum), then `jobs_wait` with what `shutdown.total_timeout` leaves after the fixed budgets of the role (soft stop, telemetry flush, and for `all` drain and HTTP grace), left out when that is 0. `Runtime.Stop` cancels the running jobs at once when its context ends and waits again on a second call. | River escalates to the hard stop after `SoftStopTimeout`; a single step with exactly that budget would leave no time for the cancelled jobs to record their state before the pool closes, and their rows would wait an hour for River's rescuer. A budget of 0 (the whole rest) let a handler that ignores its cancelled context take the telemetry flush's budget (red team, minor). |
| M1-14/5 | 2026-10-12 | `TestShutdown_SoftTimeoutCancelsAndRetries` checks the row as `available` with attempt 0 and no recorded error after the stop, and the next run on attempt 1. | River v0.49.0 marks a job its own shutdown cancelled as interrupted: back to `available` without counting the attempt; the brief expected `retryable` with attempt 1 and an error. The acceptance criterion (cancelled after the soft timeout, runs again) holds. |
| M1-14/6 | 2026-10-12 | The panic line `job panicked` carries `bothy.job.kind` and `error.type=panic`, not the job ID; the job row records `jobs: job <kind> panicked`, never the panic value. | The attribute catalogue (signals.md 5.3) has no job ID key, and the log handlers drop unknown keys; the line lies inside the job span, and the row with its attempt is the place to find the job. A panic value can carry content. |
| M1-14/7 | 2026-10-12 | River's nightly reindexer is off (`ReindexerSchedule = NeverSchedule`); all other River defaults of v0.49.0 stay (README of `platform/jobs`). | `bothy_app` owns no index of River, so `REINDEX` could only fail every night; reindexing stays with the owner role. |
| M1-14/8 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy migrate validate` stays without a database and checks River's embedded migrations (load, numbering); the database check `ValidateMigrations` runs at the start of `bothy serve` for every role, and `status` prints the River version. | 02 "Befehle des Binarys" says `validate` needs no database; River's `Validate` needs one. Checking at the start is the counterpart of the goose version check (R-AR-16). |
| M1-14/9 | 2026-10-12 | The schema step of `migrate up` also revokes `PUBLIC` on `jobs.schema`, grants on everything that already exists besides the default privileges, and runs under a session advisory lock; `jobs.schema` must not be `public` or `information_schema` or start with `pg_`; reserved words such as `user` stay allowed. | Idempotent also for tables an earlier run created; two concurrent `migrate up` must not race on `CREATE SCHEMA` (pg `Step` contract); River tables must never land in the domain schema (R-AR-09). |
| M1-14/10 | 2026-10-12 | API details beyond the brief: `New` takes `*pg.Pool`, and `pg.Pool` gains `PGX()` for River's driver; `jobs.Config` gains `Logger`; a nil tracer means the global provider's; `Migrate` returns what it applied; `lifecycle.Runner.FailOn` watches `Failed`. `jobs.queue.*.max_workers` are bounded 1 to 10000. | River's pgx driver needs the pgx pool; the command prints what River applied; the lifecycle stays free of `platform/jobs`; 10000 is River's limit per queue. |
| M1-14/11 | 2026-10-12 | The work happens on branch `feat/M1-14-background-jobs` from `int/m1-wave3`; it is not merged locally into a base branch, the PR lane ports it. | Launcher task overrides the brief's local merge. |
| M1-14/12 | 2026-10-12 | `jobs.Migrate` and `jobs.New` for `worker` and `all` refuse a pool below two connections (`database.max_conns`) with an error naming the key. | The advisory lock of `Migrate` holds one connection while River migrates on another, and a working River client holds one for LISTEN; River waits for a connection without a deadline, so a pool of one hung until the caller's deadline (reproduced in `TestMigrateNeedsTwoConnections`). |
| M1-14/13 | 2026-10-12 | `TestQuerier_AcquireTimeout` (M1-13) takes its one held connection through `Pool.PGX()` instead of `Pool.Acquire`; a test-only change outside the brief's file list. | Under the load of `make check` the first dial took longer than the test's 50ms `database.acquire_timeout` and failed with `unavailable: the database is not reachable` before the test reached what it checks; the gate must be green for this ticket. |
| M1-14/14 | 2026-10-12 | `jobs.Migrate` takes its advisory lock by polling `pg_try_advisory_lock` with goose's cadence (`pg.MigrateLockPoll`, `pg.MigrateLockAttempts`, newly exported from the existing constants of `platform/pg`) instead of `pg_advisory_lock`. | `lock_timeout = migrate.lock_timeout` holds on every migrate connection and also ends `pg_advisory_lock`, so a second concurrent `migrate up` failed after that timeout instead of waiting as it does for goose's lock (red team minor, reproduced in `TestMigrateWaitsForAConcurrentRun`). |
| M1-14/15 | 2026-10-12 | The doc comment of `pg.Pool.PGX` says that it is meant for `platform/jobs` and that no gate enforces it; no new Semgrep rule. | The red team found the old wording promised an enforcement that does not exist; one caller today does not justify a rule outside the brief's scope. |

## M1-19 Rate limits per class

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-19/1 | 2026-10-12 | Rounding of the headers: `r` = whole tokens left after the decision, rounded down; `t` = seconds until full, rounded up, `0` when full; `Retry-After` = seconds until one token, rounded up and at least `1`. Tokens are fixed point (1/60e9 of a token, refilled by `per_minute` units per nanosecond), so every value is an exact integer division. Recorded in 06 "Rate-Limits" point 5. | Rounding down `r` and up `t` and `Retry-After` never promises a token that is not there; integer arithmetic makes the exact header tests possible without float drift. |
| M1-19/2 | 2026-10-12 | A principal, agent or host class counts an anonymous request (or one without an actor) by its client IP with the same normalisation as `rl.public_auth` (IPv6 by /64, IPv4-mapped unmapped). Keys carry a kind prefix (`ip:`, `p:`, `a:`, `h:`, `d:`, `t:`, `c:`, `w:`), and the internal key is `<class>|<key>`. Recorded in 06 "Rate-Limits" point 6. | Unauthenticated calls must neither share one bucket (one client could starve all) nor fall into a principal's bucket; the prefixes keep the IP fallback and the principal apart within a class. |
| M1-19/3 | 2026-10-12 | `rl.webhook_in` is keyed by the path value `webhook_id` of `POST /api/v1/hooks/{webhook_id}/{token}`, never by the token; the start check refuses an operation of this class without `{webhook_id}` in its path. | 05 counts per incoming webhook; the ID names it, while the token is a secret that must not sit in memory as a map key or show up in a key derivation. Someone who knows a webhook ID can exhaust its bucket before the token check; that is the price of counting before the use case and matches the purpose of the class (bounding a flood on one webhook). |
| M1-19/4 | 2026-10-12 | Eviction: at most `ratelimit.max_tracked_keys` buckets in a fixed number of shards (at least 16 per shard, at most 64 shards, shard by `hash/maphash` of the internal key with a random seed per limiter, instead of the FNV-1a the brief names, which a client can compute); the per-shard cap is the cap divided by the shards, rounded down, so the total never exceeds the key. An insert into a full shard removes every full bucket of a random sample of 8 (Go map iteration order), else the sampled bucket with the oldest last use. `Sweep` removes full buckets every `ratelimit.sweep_interval`. The limiter never denies because the table is full. Every REST key has a bounded length (IP, prefix or ID; a `webhook_id` that is not an ID is `404` before a bucket exists). Residual risk, accepted: one client that can create many distinct keys within one refill window (many `webhook_id` values or many IPv6 /64 prefixes) can make the limiter evict non-full buckets, its own included, and so reset them; the random seed means it has to flood every shard instead of one, that is more than `ratelimit.max_tracked_keys` fresh keys per refill window. | A full bucket decides like a missing one, so removing it changes nothing; the cap must survive a flood of keys without unbounded memory, and a limiter that denied on a full table would let that flood lock out every client. The seed and the key length check follow the red team of M1-19 (finding 2); a shared table per client would need a second limit layer that 05 does not define. |
| M1-19/5 | 2026-10-12 | The failed-credentials rule needs a check before the `Authenticator`, which the hook of M1-18 (called only after a failure) cannot give. `platform/httpserver` gets `Chain.SetCredentialsPrecheck` (runs only for requests with an `Authorization` header); its function returns a settle function that the stage calls with the result of the `Authenticator`. `ratelimit.Install` sets only the precheck; `SetFailedCredentialsHook` of M1-18 stays for other uses. The precheck reserves one token of `rl.public_auth` with `Allow`; an empty bucket is `429` with `RateLimit-Policy` and `RateLimit` of `rl.public_auth`. Settle keeps the token for a failure with `401` other than `token_expired` and `use_dpop_nonce` and returns it with `Limiter.Refund` otherwise (success, those two codes, an error of another kind such as `503` of the identity store). At most `burst` verifications of one client IP run per refill; more than `burst` requests with credentials of one client IP in flight at the same instant get `429` even with valid credentials. | 02 "Kette im api-Knoten" bounds the signature checks a failing client can cause; a precheck that only looked let concurrent requests all pass on one token (red team M1-19, finding 1). A server failure is not the client's fault and must not lock out an IP; returning the token keeps valid credentials free of cost. The in-flight limit is the price of the bound and is far above a normal client's parallelism at the default burst. |
| M1-19/6 | 2026-10-12 | Files of `platform/httpserver` outside the brief's scope change: `Operation.RateLimitClass` (copied by `wire.HTTPOperation`, held by `TestHTTPOperationCarriesEveryField`), `SetCredentialsPrecheck`, and `httpservertest.Options.Setup`, which runs on the chain before the routes are mounted so tests install the limiter as `internal/wire` does. `platform/ratelimit` depends on `httpserver` and `otel` in `architecture/modules.yaml`. | The stage must read the class of the route from the context, and `routes.Operation` cannot be imported by the platform layer (M1-18/2). The harness had no way to set hooks before `Wrap` seals the chain. |
| M1-19/7 | 2026-10-12 | The key kind table is hand-written in `ratelimit/classes.go`, because `actions.yaml` holds the key column only as German prose; `TestKeyKinds_MatchChapter05` parses the generated table of 05 and maps each wording to a kind. The kind list of the brief gets `KeyHost` for `rl.host` ("Rechner"); `rl.terminal` and `rl.import` are principal classes as the table says (the person, the owner). | The brief allows the hand-written table with this test; without `KeyHost` the class of the host service would have no key. |
| M1-19/8 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the keys: `ratelimit.<class>.per_minute` and `.burst` from 1 to 1000000 (`ratelimit.MaxLimit`), `ratelimit.max_tracked_keys` from 1 to 100000000, `ratelimit.sweep_interval` from 1s to 1h. A value outside stops the start with exit code 2 (configuration error); `ratelimit.New` checks the same bounds again. | Zero would disable a class or the sweeper (a ticker needs a positive interval); the upper bound keeps `burst` x 60e9 far inside int64. |
| M1-19/9 | 2026-10-12 | The middleware fails closed: an operation without a class, with an unknown class, with a gateway class (`rl.typing`, `rl.gateway_connect`) or without a key answers `500 internal_error` and never reaches the handler; the start check of `internal/wire` (`InstallRateLimits`) refuses these operations before the listener opens. `bothy.ratelimit.rejected` counts every denial of `Allow` (REST, gateway, failed credentials) and every refusal of the precheck. | Serving an operation without a limit would bypass the requirement silently. One counting point keeps the metric complete for the gateway tickets. |
| M1-19/10 | 2026-10-12 | Responses written before `StageRateLimit` (a `401` of `StageAuthenticate`, the precheck `429` aside) carry no `RateLimit` headers of the route's class. | The order of 02 "Kette im api-Knoten" puts authentication before the rate limit; the class key of a principal is known only afterwards. |
| M1-19/11 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-19-rate-limits` from `int/m1-wave4` (`76b79a5`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. Text added to the German chapter 06 is English. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's merge step; everything added is English (M1-23/9). |
| M1-19/12 | 2026-10-12 | A `webhook_id` that is not an ID (06: UUIDv7) gets `404 not_found` in `StageRateLimit`, before the validator, which would answer a malformed path parameter with `400`; the key uses the canonical form of the ID. A panicking `Authenticator` settles the reserved `rl.public_auth` token like a server failure (returned), then the recover step answers `500`. For M1-34: everything except a counting `401` returns the token, so the identity ticket must report a failed expensive proof as a counting `401`, else the rule only bounds concurrent proofs. | The stage must not create a bucket for a value that names no webhook, and an unknown webhook is a 404 in the use case as well, so the answer leaks nothing; red team M1-19 round 2 found the 404 path, the panic path and the M1-34 dependency. |

## Integration int/m1-wave5

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W5/1 | 2026-10-12 | M1-21, M1-24, M1-25, M1-27, M1-14 and M1-19 enter as one squash each onto `76b79a5` (`int/m1-wave4`), in this order, each with only its own commits (M1-14 with `ca30b63..c32e4e7`, the others with `76b79a5..<head>`). Append-only files (DECISIONS, CHANGELOGs, `migrations.sha256`, the privileges table, `sqlc.yaml`, the cross-check list of `internal/wire/configchecks.go`, the start step table and order comment of `internal/wire`) hold both sides; a module that two tickets give adapter vendors lists both (`authz`: `[otel, pgx]`). Generated files (`.go-arch-lint.yml`, sqlc models, `config-schema.json`, the configuration reference, the catalogue copies) come from the generators of the head. | The tickets were built and red teamed separately on the same base; a union keeps each ticket's behaviour, and generated files are only evidence while they equal a fresh generation. |
| INT-W5/2 | 2026-10-12 | M1-24 (`00004_audit_events.sql`) and M1-27 (`00004_authz_groups_grants.sql`) both added migration version 4. The migration of M1-27, the later ticket of this wave, becomes `00005_authz_groups_grants.sql` with unchanged content and checksum; `migrations.sha256`, the authz README, the server CHANGELOG and `authzVersion` in `migrations/authz_groups_grants_test.go` follow. | Before the fix the head refused its own migration set (`TestEmbeddedMigrationsValidate`: "duplicate version 4", every database test and `bothy migrate up` red); ADR 0007 rule 4 has the branch merged second renumber. |
| INT-W5/3 | 2026-10-12 | M1-15 (`instance.go`) and M1-14 (`jobs.go`) both declared `wire.MigrationSteps`, with different signatures. `MigrationSteps(pool, fsys)` of M1-15 stays as it is (the instance step after goose; `pgtest.WithSteps` uses it for the test templates); the River step of M1-14 moves into the new `MigrateUpSteps(pool, fsys, schema, result)`, which returns River's step before goose and the steps of `MigrationSteps` after it. `bothy migrate` calls `MigrateUpSteps`; `TestMigrateUpSteps` holds the order (river before, instance after). | Without the fix the module did not compile. The test templates stay without River's tables, because the serve tests of M1-14 add them themselves (`newServeDB`) and `TestServeAbortsWithoutRiverTables` needs a database without them; M1-15's comment already named this place for River's step. |
| INT-W5/4 | 2026-10-12 | `TestQuerier_AcquireTimeout` keeps the M1-14 form (M1-14/13: the held connection through `Pool.PGX()` without the acquire timeout) instead of the retry loop of M1-23/11; both fixed the same flake, the test's three checks are unchanged. | One fix for one cause; the M1-14 form does not depend on a retry window, and the now unused `pgxpool` import goes. |
| INT-W5/5 | 2026-10-12 | Tests of one ticket that broke on a rule of another are adapted, the code under test is unchanged: the serve tests of M1-15 (`instance_test.go`) and M1-21 (`transport_test.go`) that reach the jobs step use `newServeDB` (River's tables, M1-14 makes them a start condition); `TestJobsConfigBindsDefaults` (M1-14) passes `server.public_url` (required since M1-18); `TestMigrate_RiverInOwnSchema`, `TestMigrateUpBootstrap` and `TestMigrateStatusOutput` expect the River line followed by the lines derived from the embedded migration set (M1-26 helpers) instead of the one migration of M1-14's base. | Before the fix these tests failed on the head (`connection refused` on `/readyz` after the start ended at the jobs step, `server.public_url: is required`, a stdout with five migrations where one was expected). |
| INT-W5/6 | 2026-10-12 | `capNames` (`caps.go`) and `levelNames` (`levels.go`) of `internal/authz/domain` (M1-27) carry a `//nolint:goconst` with its reason; the literals stay. | goconst counts per package, and the generated catalogue metadata of M1-25 (`catalog_gen.go`) in the same package writes `manage` 14 times and `read` 58 times, so `make lint` failed on the head (2 goconst findings) although each ticket passed alone; generated files are only excluded from the report, not from the count. A level name is not the action effect `EffectRead`, so a shared constant would couple two vocabularies; Sonar counts per file and sees no duplicate. |

## M1-16 Event log with commit-ordered sequence

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-16/1 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-16-event-log` in its own worktree from the head of `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done"). |
| M1-16/2 | 2026-10-12 | A payload must be smaller than `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` (a payload of exactly the limit is rejected), as 04 "Regeln für die Nutzlast" item 2 ("kleiner als") and the fixed requirement 16 of the brief ("below") say; 03 ("bis") and the design note ("at most") read as an upper bound the strict rule also keeps. | The core contract chapters disagree by one byte; the strict reading satisfies every text. |
| M1-16/3 | 2026-10-12 | The appender also rejects a payload whose strings or keys contain U+0000. | jsonb cannot store U+0000 (SQLSTATE 22P05); the producer would otherwise get a database error inside the hook instead of a kind internal error at the append. |
| M1-16/4 | 2026-10-12 | `Event.Envelope` and the mapping rules live in the port `internal/sync`; `domain/` holds the validation, the partition arithmetic and the gap check. | The brief asks for the method on the port type and for the rules in domain; the port may import only kernel packages (`.go-arch-lint.yml`), so it cannot call domain. |
| M1-16/5 | 2026-10-12 | `DeliveryFields` carries `SyncEpoch` as well (`sync_epoch`, webhooks only, ADR 0059). | `EventEnvelope` in openapi.yaml has the member, and `TestEnvelope_FieldsMatchOpenAPI` requires the envelope members to equal its properties. |
| M1-16/6 | 2026-10-12 | The dedicated connection is `(*pg.Pool).DialDedicated(ctx, applicationName)` instead of a free function `pg.DialDedicated`. | A connection with the configuration and password source of the pool needs both; the pool already holds them. |
| M1-16/7 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.MigrateUpSteps(pool, fsys, MigrateUpConfig{JobsSchema, Sync}, result)` adds the after-step `event_log_partitions` behind the instance step; `wire.MigrationSteps`, which `pgtest.WithSteps` uses for templates, keeps only the instance step, so test databases have no partitions and the event log tests create theirs with their own size. | `bothy migrate up` must use the configured values (brief scope item 9); pgtest's step function gets no configuration. |
| M1-16/8 | 2026-10-12 | The new partition table is `LIKE event_log INCLUDING DEFAULTS INCLUDING CONSTRAINTS`. | ATTACH PARTITION requires the CHECK constraints of the parent on the attached table; with `INCLUDING DEFAULTS` alone it fails. |
| M1-16/9 | 2026-10-12 | `sync_event_log_ensure_partitions` locks `event_log` in `SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE` mode before it reads the bounds; `sync_event_log_drop_expired` stops at the first partition it keeps, so only a prefix of partitions is ever dropped. | Two concurrent runs (job and `bothy migrate up`) would otherwise create the same table twice; `occurred_at` is the start of a transaction and need not grow with `seq`, and a hole in the retained range would break `min_retained_seq` (04 "resync_required"). |
| M1-16/10 | 2026-10-12 | The rows go in with one multi-row insert from arrays, and `pg_notify` runs in the same statement (a data-modifying CTE), not with sqlc `:copyfrom` and not as a second statement. | Every round trip under the counter lock lowers the commit rate (`perf.event_commit_min_per_s`); one statement is one round trip, COPY would need a second one for the notification. |
| M1-16/11 | 2026-10-12 | Without a valid span context the correlation ID of the request in `kernel/reqmeta` (if it is a UUID) becomes `correlation_id`; `trace_context` stays empty. Without a span and outside a request both are NULL. | 03 `event_log.correlation_id`: a request without traceparent gets an ID from the server; `platform/httpserver` keeps that ID in reqmeta, the same the response and the audit entry carry. |
| M1-16/12 | 2026-10-12 | The error line of an insert without a partition carries `bothy.event.seq` (the first seq) and `error.type` (`23514`); the whole range is in the error text. | The log handlers keep only catalogue attributes (`platform/otel/attr`), and the catalogue has no second seq attribute; adding one is a telemetry change outside this ticket. |
| M1-16/13 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the new keys: `sync.event_log_partition_size` at least 1, `sync.event_retention` at least 1s, `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` 3 to 1048576, `sync.log_poll_interval` at least 1ms, `sync.tail_batch_size` 1 to 2147483647 (a PostgreSQL int limit), `sync.partition_maintenance_interval` at least 1s, `sync.event_log_partitions_ahead` 1 to 1000. | The chapters give only defaults; `{}` is the smallest payload, 0 partitions ahead would let the next commit miss a partition, and a payload limit above `http.max_json_body_bytes` would never be reached by a request. |
| M1-16/14 | 2026-10-12 | The tail's start step waits until `Run` has read the head (`Tail.Started`) before the public listener opens; a tail that ends with an error calls `Runner.Fail`, and its stop step `event_tail` runs in `PhaseEventTail` without its own budget. | 02 "Start" step 8 sets `local_head` before the listener; the stop only cancels a loop. |
| M1-16/15 | 2026-10-12 | ADR 0009 is not changed; its confirmation is held by `TestEventLog_ConcurrentWritersGapless` and `TestTail_ListenConnectionLossLosesNothing`. | The brief extends only ADR 0052, 0053 and 0063. |
| M1-16/16 | 2026-10-12 | `make perf` measured `perf.event_commit_min_per_s` below the threshold on this machine (the database runs in a Colima VM, one round trip about 0.4ms from the test process); the gate stays as ADR 0063 defines it and the numbers are in the report, not in docs. | Under the counter lock every unit spends about three round trips and the commit, so the rate on this machine is bound by the VM network, not by the pattern; the reference profile runs PostgreSQL locally (02 "Konfigurationswerte", `perf.reference_profile`). |
| M1-16/17 | 2026-10-12 | The module README of sync keeps its generated German sections (purpose, port, dependencies, tables, actions, events, jobs), which `make register-check` compares with the register, and adds the English sections of this ticket. | Text added by this ticket is English; the generated sections are not this ticket's text. |
| M1-16/18 | 2026-10-12 | The appender rejects numbers with an exponent, invalid UTF-8 and unpaired surrogate escapes, and the reads compact the jsonb text before they hand payloads to the sinks. | Red team round 1 (M1): jsonb writes `1e300` out in full, so a checked payload could be stored and delivered far above `sync.event_payload_max_bytes`; without exponents the compact form of the normalised jsonb is never larger than the checked bytes. |
| M1-16/19 | 2026-10-12 | The partition maintenance runs `ensure` and `drop_expired` in two units of work; an error of the drop is `ErrDropDeferred` with the result of the first unit, the job is retried by River, and the partition step of `bothy migrate up` ignores it. | Red team round 1 (M2): a DETACH waiting behind a long reader rolled back the partitions created in the same unit, and appends ran out of partitions. |
| M1-16/20 | 2026-10-12 | `sync_event_log_drop_expired` clamps `p_head` to `max(seq)` and `p_cutoff` to `now()`; `sync_event_log_ensure_partitions` refuses a size above 10^12 and more than 1000 partitions ahead, and `sync.event_log_partition_size` has the maximum 10^12. | Red team round 1 (Minor, hardening): bothy_app could drop the head partition with arguments of its choice, and an unbounded size overflows bigint in `p_head + p_ahead * p_size`. |
| M1-16/21 | 2026-10-12 | A sink error while the context of `Tail.Run` has ended is the orderly end of `Run` (nil), not a fatal error; a stored payload `null` renders as `{}` in the envelope. | Red team round 1 (Minor): a sink that honours the context would otherwise turn every shutdown into exit code 1. |
| M1-16/22 | 2026-10-12 | Numbers with more than 131072 digits before or 16383 digits after the point are rejected at the append; only a lock timeout of the drop unit becomes `ErrDropDeferred`, any other drop error is returned unchanged. | Red team round 2 (M1-rest): such numbers passed the appender and failed the unit at the insert with SQLSTATE 22003; a permanent drop failure must not pass silently as deferred. |
| M1-16/23 | 2026-10-12 | Long digit runs (17 to 20 digits) never appear as literals in tests: names and limits are built (`fmt.Sprintf("%019d", ...)`, `math.MaxInt64` as a parameter), and the generic white-label scan (`tools/denylist`, `make test` and `make denylist`) runs on the working tree before every commit of this ticket. | Red team rounds 1 and 2 (B1): the generic rule for account and card numbers matched partition names and the bigint maximum twice; the gate that catches the class exists, the gap was running it before the commit. |

## M1-28 Rights snapshot, cache and authorize

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-28/D4 | 2026-10-12 | Visibility per resource kind as the reading of 07 section 4.3 for resources outside the structure tree: instance for every authenticated principal or a `Public` action; space with read on the space or on one of its channels; channel-path kinds (channel, thread, message, reaction, pin, run, run_step, component, interaction, agent_session, file, incoming_webhook, command) for dm participants only in a dm channel, otherwise with read on the path; grant for owners or with read on a space or channel target (instance and host targets: owner only); agent for owners, the agent itself or read on a channel it operates; user and principal for people and apps, or the principal itself; group for owners or members in effect; device, auth_session, api_token, passkey, push_registration, draft, gdpr_request and dev_environment for the owner of the resource, or an effective owner when the requirement names `owner`; app, webhook_delivery, import_source and host for owners. Any other kind (terminal_session included) is an internal error. Invisible is `not_found` with `no_grant`, `not_self` for the principal-owned kinds and `acting_for_insufficient` when only the acting-for intersection removed the visibility. On a space, `Level(read)` means "space visible". | 07 section 4.3 lists the rules for the tree and names the owned resources only by example; one table in `domain/visibility.go` makes every kind explicit and fails closed for a kind it does not know. The space rule was found by AZ-P8: a person who reads only a channel of a space must see the space (05 `space.read`). |

## M3-01 Object storage port with file system driver

The brief names these rows D-M3-01-1 to D-M3-01-13; they follow the numbering of this log (M3-01/1 to M3-01/13), and M3-01/14 onwards are rulings of the build.

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M3-01/1 | 2026-10-12 | `ObjectStore` is an outgoing port of the application layer in `internal/files/app`, with its value types (`ByteRange`, `ObjectInfo`, `Part`, `PresignedPart`) and errors. The root package `internal/files` stays the incoming port for other modules, which never see `ObjectStore`. Key schema and key grammar are pure functions in `internal/files/domain`. The conformance suite is the test helper package `internal/files/adapters/filestest`, imported only by `_test.go` files; `TestFilestestNotInBinary` holds it out of `cmd/bothy`. | 02 "5. Schichten im Modul": the application layer holds outgoing ports as interfaces, adapters implement them; 02 "6. Code-Layout" places test helpers in `adapters/<modul>test`. |
| M3-01/2 | 2026-10-12 | Error contract of the port: `ErrInvalidKey` (the same value as `domain.ErrInvalidKey`, checked before any storage call), `ErrNotFound`, `ErrInvalidRange`, `ErrSizeMismatch`, `ErrUploadNotFound`, `ErrInvalidPart` (the upload stays open, the list shape is checked before any service call), `ErrNotSupported`. `Delete` of a missing object and `AbortMultipart` of an unknown, aborted or completed upload return nil. Every other error is a storage failure (`503 unavailable`); errors wrap the cause, name operation and key, never contain object bytes, and a refused key is not repeated. Recorded in 12 "4.1". | 12 "4.1" and "12": a missing object counts as deleted, so retried cleanup jobs converge; callers decide `503` against a client error by sentinel only, and a key a caller passed may carry anything. |
| M3-01/3 | 2026-10-12 | `Get` takes at most one byte range `{Offset, Length}`; a range whose end lies beyond the object ends at the last byte, `Offset < 0`, `Length < 1` and `Offset` at or beyond the size give `ErrInvalidRange`. | 12 "9.3" supports exactly one range, and an HTTP range past the end is cut there; an offset beyond the end has no byte to return (`416` for M3-05). |
| M3-01/4 | 2026-10-12 | `ValidateKey` is one grammar for all drivers: segments of `[a-z0-9-]` joined by single `/`, no leading or trailing `/`. `filepath.Clean(key) == key` holds for every accepted key; `FuzzValidateKey` holds the grammar. | ADR 0010: the key schema is the same in both drivers, so a key that works on one works on the other, and no key reaches outside the root. |
| M3-01/5 | 2026-10-12 | On the file system `CreateMultipart` accepts only original keys, creates `uploads/<file_id>/`, is idempotent and returns the upload ID `""`; `UploadPart` and `CompleteMultipart` need the ID `""` and that directory, else `ErrUploadNotFound`. While an upload is open, its parts are regular objects under `uploads/<file_id>/<n>`; the conformance suite never relies on that. The driver takes no lock. | 03 `file_upload_sessions.storage_upload_id` holds an upload ID only for the S3 driver, and the place of the parts follows from the key (12 "3.2"); clients upload only originals in parts; the session row of M3-03 serializes completion against late parts. |
| M3-01/6 | 2026-10-12 | The file system part ETag is the lower-case hex SHA-256 of the part bytes in double quotes, computed while writing; `CompleteMultipart` reads each listed part exactly once into one temporary file, checks its hash against the ETag (`ErrInvalidPart` on a mismatch, the upload stays), and removes `uploads/<file_id>/` with all parts, listed or not; a failure of that removal after the rename does not fail the completion. | `UploadPartResult.etag` is required for both drivers, and the form equals the `ETag` header of 12 "9.3"; one read per part follows R-FI-04; S3 also discards unlisted parts; a retry after a reported cleanup failure would find parts missing and refuse a finished object, while the `Delete` of the original removes leftover parts (M3-01/18). |
| M3-01/7 | 2026-10-12 | Temporary files of writes lie in `<root>/.tmp` with mode `0600` and a random name; every failed write removes its own. | `.tmp` starts with a dot, which no key may contain, so no key addresses a temporary file; the same file system makes the rename atomic. |
| M3-01/8 | 2026-10-12 | The store keeps an `os.Root` of the root and in addition follows no symbolic link at all: each existing path component is checked with `Lstat` through the root before an operation and again before the rename. `os.Root` follows a link inside the root even with `O_NOFOLLOW` (Go `rootOpenFileNolog` resolves `ELOOP` itself, except with `O_CREATE|O_EXCL`), so a read compares the opened file with the checked one (`os.SameFile`) and on a difference checks the path again: a link there is a storage failure, another regular file is a concurrent replace and a valid read (`TestGetRefusesLinkSwappedBeforeOpen`). Unix-only file; on other systems `Open` refuses every root. A link found is a storage failure, not `ErrNotFound`. | 12 "4.2": the driver follows no symbolic link; `os.Root` alone keeps operations inside the root but would follow links within it; a storage failure makes the cause visible to the operator. Residual window: a process that can write into the root could swap a component for a link between the check and the call; `os.Root` still keeps that inside the root, and only the server user can write there (start check). Such a process could also put a link in and replace it by a regular file again between the open and the second check; the read then returns the bytes of the link target, still an object inside the root. |
| M3-01/9 | 2026-10-12 | `fs.Open` checks the root: absolute and clean, exists, `Lstat` shows a directory and not a link, owned by the effective user, permission bits exactly `0700` (no setuid, setgid or sticky bit), the opened handle is the same directory as the checked one. It creates `<root>/.tmp` with mode `0700` when missing, refuses a `.tmp` that is not a directory of the same user with mode exactly `0700`, never creates the root and never removes entries of `.tmp`. Errors name `file.fs.root` and the expected state, never the path. | The same start check as a server-owned key directory (M1-32); a missing root means a wrong mount, and entries of `.tmp` may belong to another process on the same root. |
| M3-01/10 | 2026-10-12 | `Put` on an existing key replaces it atomically; the drivers do not enforce "Objekte werden nie überschrieben". | The module enforces it through the key schema (a changed original gets the next revision) and the unique `files.object_key`; a retried job may rewrite a key that no committed row names yet. |
| M3-01/11 | 2026-10-12 | `Delete` removes the file, syncs the directory, then removes `f/<aa>/<bb>/<file_id>` or `uploads/<file_id>` if it is empty now ("not empty" and "already gone" are ignored); the fan-out levels stay. A writer whose directory vanished before the rename creates it again and renames again (see M3-01/15). Deleting the last part of an open upload removes `uploads/<file_id>/` and so ends the upload. | Without it every deleted file would leave a directory behind, and the server never lists to find them (12 "4.1"). |
| M3-01/12 | 2026-10-12 | Cross-check `file_fs_root_absolute` on `file.storage.driver` and `file.fs.root`: with `fs` the root must satisfy `filepath.IsAbs` and `filepath.Clean(root) == root`; with `s3` nothing is checked. Recorded in 02 "13. Konfigurationsladen" item 4. | A relative root would depend on the working directory of the process; with `s3` the root is unused. |
| M3-01/13 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.FilesObjectStore` refuses the driver `s3` with `config: file.storage.driver: driver s3 is not supported by this server version` until M3-02 replaces the branch. No role calls `FilesObjectStore` yet. | As M1-07 did for the secret schemes `azkv` and `awssm`; M3-03 opens the store in the roles together with the image directory, because opening it at start now would stop the stack. |
| M3-01/14 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M3-01-object-storage-fs` from `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. Text added to the German chapters 12 and 02 and to ADR 0010 is English, as is the README addition of `files`. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's merge step and over "German like the chapter"; everything added is English (M1-23/9, M1-19/11). |
| M3-01/15 | 2026-10-12 | The rename of a write repeats (directory created again) as long as it fails because a concurrent `Delete` removed the emptied file directory, instead of the single retry of the brief; it stops when the rename succeeds, fails for another reason, the temporary file is gone or the context ends. "Vanished" is `ENOENT`, `EINVAL` from the rename, or a directory that is missing now; `mkdirs` creates a component again that vanished between `Mkdir` and `Lstat`. A directory sync after the rename that finds the directory gone is no error. | `TestTwoStoresShareRoot` (two stores, parallel `Put`, `Get` and `Delete` on keys of the same files) failed with a single retry on macOS: renameat returns `EINVAL`, not `ENOENT`, when a concurrent rmdir removes the target directory during the call (reproduced in a probe), and two removals can fall into one write. Each repetition needs another delete that empties the directory in that window, so no count is needed; a count would be a threshold without a configuration key. |
| M3-01/16 | 2026-10-12 | `*fs.Store` has `Close` (releases the root handle) besides the port; `FilesObjectStore` returns the store as `filesapp.ObjectStore`, which implements `io.Closer` for the driver `fs`, and never a typed nil. | The `os.Root` holds a file descriptor; the port has no lifecycle operation (12 "4.1"), so the owner of the store (M3-03) closes it through `io.Closer`. |
| M3-01/17 | 2026-10-12 | Every directory a write creates is synced in its parent (`TestNewDirectoriesAreSynced`), beyond the sync of the target directory that the brief names. `CompleteMultipart` checks the shape of the part list before any file system call, also before the upload check, and the conformance suite holds it (an empty list on an unknown upload is `ErrInvalidPart`). | Code review M3-01 (findings 2 and 3): without the parent sync a crash after a successful `Put` can lose the entry of a new file directory on file systems without ordered metadata; the list check first keeps both drivers equal (design note 3). A writer that finds a directory already created by a concurrent writer does not sync its parent again; the creator does, and an extra fsync per existing level on every write would cost more than the window between another writer's `Mkdir` and its parent sync. |
| M3-01/18 | 2026-10-12 | `Delete` of an original key also removes `uploads/<file_id>/` with any parts in it (`TestCompleteSurvivesCleanupFailure`), also when the object itself is already gone. | Code re-review M3-01: when `CompleteMultipart` cannot remove the parts after the rename, nothing else would ever remove them (a completed session never expires, the server never lists), and deleting the file must remove all its bytes (12 "12", also for GDPR erasure). An upload in parts exists only for a file that has no committed original yet, and the delete of a failed upload wants its parts gone too. |
| M3-01/19 | 2026-10-12 | Red team M3-01 (verdict JA, five minors): `PresignUploadPart` checks the part number (`ErrInvalidPart` for `n < 1`) before it answers `ErrNotSupported`, as the port contract says for every driver; `TestKeyBuildersTakeOnlyIDsAndNumbers` finds every exported `...Key` function of `internal/files/domain` in the source, so a new builder cannot escape the parameter check. Not changed, by decision: an upload directory left by a failed cleanup after `CompleteMultipart` still accepts parts until the original is deleted (the session row of M3-03 refuses parts and completion after `completed`); a file system upload is bound to the file ID, not to the revision of the key (only `orig-1` is uploaded in parts, 12 "3.1"); `Put` on a part key creates `uploads/<file_id>` (parts are objects, M3-01/5, and no caller writes part keys with `Put`). | Design note 3 puts the part number rule into the port for both drivers, and design note 11 only forbids file system calls; the other three cases need a caller that M3-01 does not have, and guarding them in the driver would duplicate the session state of M3-03. |

## M3-17 Notification decision

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-17-1 | 2026-10-12 | The decision per recipient lives in `services/server/internal/notify/domain` and is split into `ResolveLevel`, `InQuietHours`, `Decide`, `Flush` and `ReleaseDelayed`. They take facts and `Now` in and never call `authorize`, read a clock, load a time zone or read configuration: `Allowed` is the `push.deliver` result the caller gathered with current rights, each quiet-hour rule carries its loaded `*time.Location`, and the caller passes the values of `notify.coalesce_window`, `notify.mobile_delay_when_desktop_active` and `notify.user_default_level` and the personal switches resolved with their defaults. `TestDomainPurity` and `make arch` hold it. Place: the package, its README. | 13 12 point 3 and 02 5 "Schichten im Modul": the domain holds pure functions; the rules are proven by tables instead of by PostgreSQL, a clock or a provider, and M3-18, M3-19 and M3-23 call one rule set instead of re-implementing it. |
| D-M3-17-2 | 2026-10-12 | Recipient guards and kinds without thread: the author never gets `message`, `mention` or `dm` for their own message; `run_done`, `run_failed` and `input_needed` reach only the person who triggered the run (which makes "`input_needed` (eigener Lauf)" of 13 4.3 hold); `security` and `admin` have no thread, so no level, no activity in a thread and no window apply to them (`ThreadID` must be zero, `Window` is ignored), quiet hours do. A `dm` hint requires a DM channel, and `message`, `mention` and the run kinds require a channel that is not one; a mismatch is invalid input. For `security` and `admin`, `Allowed` is the recipient rule of 13 2 as the caller evaluates it. Place: 13 2 and 4.4. | The domain does not classify events into kinds (M3-19 does), but a wrong caller must fail closed. A message in a DM channel is `dm` also when it mentions r, because both kinds need the same level and go at once; 05 4.9 defines `push.deliver` on a thread only. |
| D-M3-17-3 | 2026-10-12 | `muted_until` counts only on the chosen own row (thread, channel or space, the first present) and only while it is strictly after now; at now it has passed and the row's stored level applies again. A `muted_until` on a row that was not chosen has no effect; a row stored as `mute` stays `mute` after its `muted_until` passed. `EffectiveLevel` carries `Level`, `Source` (the step of 13 3.1) and `Muted`. Place: `level.go`. | 13 3.1 "bei der gewählten Zeile ... in der Zukunft": "in the future" is strict; an inner row decides even when an outer one is muted, so a thread the person follows keeps its own level. |
| D-M3-17-4 | 2026-10-12 | Quiet hours: start and end are whole minutes (`HH:MM`, 0 to 1439), the wall time is compared to the second; `start_local = end_local` is a window of 24 hours from `start_local` on each set weekday (screen D9 "ganztags" is `00:00` to `00:00`); the caller loads the zone of the row or `users.time_zone`; every rule, also a disabled one, must be well formed; a delayed push keeps the `Quiet` flag of its decision. Place: 13 3.4, `quiet.go`. | The schema stores `HH:MM`; second precision makes 06:59:59 quiet and 07:00:00 not. 13 3.4 point 3 already covers `end_local <= start_local`, so equal times are a full day, not an empty window. The gateway copy of the same hint went out quietly at once, and the delay is at most `notify.mobile_delay_when_desktop_active`. A broken row fails closed instead of being skipped silently. |
| D-M3-17-5 | 2026-10-12 | Window rules: the window of (recipient, thread) is open while `Now < End` and closed at `Now == End`; a kind with thread that sends while no window is open opens one until `Now + notify.coalesce_window`, whatever its kind; `message` in an open window is collected; urgent kinds in an open window send and leave the window unchanged; a window length of zero opens none. `Flush` (kind `message` only) gives `nothing_collected` for nothing collected and `read` for an unread count of zero or less; otherwise allowed, level, activity, quiet and route run again at the flush time, `Author` and `Window` are ignored, and a send carries the unread count of 03 `read_states` and opens the next window; a flush that sends nothing leaves the window closed. Place: 13 4.5, `decide.go`, `flush.go`. | 13 1 point 3 "Ein Hinweis je Thread und Fenster" must hold under a steady stream, and the first message after a quiet period must still go at once (R-PN-06). Rights, level and quiet hours may change during a window; a bundle has no single author. |
| D-M3-17-6 | 2026-10-12 | Activity and routes: a device is active in the thread with a connection that is not idle and whose focus holds the thread, on any platform, and that stops the hint on every device; the desktop is active with a `macos` or `windows` device connected and not idle. Routes follow the order of `Devices`: a connection (idle or not) gets `gateway`; otherwise a device with registrations gets `push_delayed` (`NotBefore = Now + notify.mobile_delay_when_desktop_active`) if it is `ios` or `android`, the desktop is active and the delay is above zero, else `push`; a device with neither is left out. No route at all gives `none` with `no_route` and opens no window. `ReleaseDelayed` drops a delayed push exactly when the read position (the greater of `last_read_seq` and `threads.unread_floor_seq`) reaches the hint's `thread_seq`; a hint without a message is always released. Place: 13 4.4 and 4.6, `decide.go`, `delay.go`. | 13 4.4 and 4.6 name the conditions; an idle desktop window still gets `notification.created` but does not count as someone watching. A hint that reaches no device must not open a window, or the next message would be collected and never shown. |
| D-M3-17-7 | 2026-10-12 | Invalid input: `ResolveLevel`, `InQuietHours`, `Decide` and `Flush` validate first and return `errs.New(errs.CodeInternalError, ...)` with the zero result (for `Decide` and `Flush` outcome `none`, no routes) for an unknown kind, level or platform; a zero `Now`; a negative window length or delay; a kind with thread and a zero `ThreadID`, or `security` or `admin` with one; an empty or unknown instance default for a kind with thread; the DM mismatch of D-M3-17-2; a zero or duplicate device ID, a zero registration ID or a zero thread ID in a focus; a malformed quiet-hour rule; for `Flush` a kind other than `message` or a negative `Collected`. The error text names the field, never a value. `Outcome` is an integer enum whose zero value is `none`, so the zero `Decision` never sends. Place: the package. | Fail closed: a broken input never yields `send` or `collect`. The caller built the facts from its own tables, so a broken fact is a server fault (`internal_error`), not a client error. |
| D-M3-17-8 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M3-17-notify-decision` from `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. Text added to the German chapter 13 and the German notify README is English. The base has no section 10 "Rulings der Bau-Etappen" in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket like the M1 rulings, with the IDs the brief names. A test helper file `fixtures_test.go` holds the shared fixtures of the test files the brief lists. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's merge step and language; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |

## M2-23 Generated TypeScript and Python clients

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2-23/1 | 2026-10-12 | Layout and names: Python distribution `bothy-sdk`, import package `bothy_sdk` in `sdk/python/src/bothy_sdk/`, generated core in the subpackage `bothy_sdk.generated`; npm package `bothy-sdk` (`"private": true`, `"type": "module"`), generated types in `sdk/typescript/src/generated/openapi.ts`. `pyproject.toml` carries `Private :: Do Not Upload`. The Python project has no build backend yet (`[tool.uv] package = false`; tests and mypy read `src/`); the release chain of the SDK components (M7) adds it. The names are a new kind in 02 6 "Stellen mit dem Produktnamen". | A directory named `generated` is already excluded by Sonar and exempt from the SPDX header check, so no gate configuration changes. Working names until OQ-17; V1-06 renames them with the other listed places. A build backend would be a further unlocked download (build dependencies are not in `uv.lock`) for an artifact nothing builds before M7. |
