## Integration int/m1-wave7

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W7/1 | 2026-10-12 | M1-20, M1-29, M1-33, M2-01, M3-02 and M2-32 enter as one squash each onto `785916c` (`int/m1-wave6`), in this order, each with only its own commits (`785916c..<head>`; M2-32 was built on `int/m1-wave5`, so `46d20a0..2e98d2e`). Append-only files (DECISIONS, both CHANGELOGs, the configuration reference, the privileges table of the migrations test, `migrations.sha256`, `renovate.json`) hold both sides. `internal/wire/sync.go` holds the idempotency store and cleanup of M1-20 next to the worker consumers of M2-01 in one `Sync` and one `SyncConfig`; the files rule of the arch lint config carries `platform-jsoncanon` (M1-20) and `canUse: [v-awssdk]` (M3-02); the config checks list `file_s3` (M3-02) before `consumer_alert_below_retention` (M2-01); the empty sources keep `PersonalAgents` (M1-29) and drop the empty credential source that M1-33 replaces. Where M2-23 (already on the base) and M2-32 made the same change (bandit exclusions, mypy per project, the coverage rows of `docs/quality/gates.md` and chapter 23, the OSV allowlist test), the head keeps the base version, which covers M2-32; `quality.coverage.python_min_percent` reads every Python project under `sdk/` and `plugins/` (M2-32). Generated files (`.go-arch-lint.yml`, sqlc models, `config-schema.json`, the configuration reference, the catalogue copies) equal a fresh generation on the head. | The tickets were built and red teamed separately; the union keeps the behaviour of each. The version of M2-23 was reviewed first and is the superset of the M2-32 change (it also excludes generated code everywhere and runs mypy quietly). |
| INT-W7/2 | 2026-10-12 | M1-20 (`00007_sync_idempotency_keys.sql`), M1-33 (`00007_identity_devices.sql`) and M2-01 (`00007_event_consumer_cursors.sql`) all added migration version 7. M1-20 keeps 7; M1-33 becomes `00008_identity_devices.sql` and M2-01 `00009_event_consumer_cursors.sql`, both with unchanged content and checksum. `migrations.sha256`, the identity and sync READMEs and the server CHANGELOG name the new files; the rulings M1-33 and D-M2-01-1 keep the name the ticket wrote, as a record of the ticket. | goose refuses a set with a duplicate version; ADR 0007 rule 4 has the branch merged later renumber, and the ticket order of the wave decides. None of the three migrations is released. |
| INT-W7/3 | 2026-10-12 | M2-01 replaced the `r.Add` list of `register` in `internal/wire/serve.go` with `components()`; M1-33 added its step `identity` to the old list. The head adds `step("identity", s.startIdentity)` to `components()` right after `authz` (step 6d), and `TestStartSequenceModuleSteps` holds audit, sync, authz, identity and routes in this order for every role. The request validator of M1-33 (`sync.OnceValues`) uses the import name `stdsync` that M2-01 gave the standard library package, because `sync` names `internal/sync` there. | Without the step the device handlers of M1-33 would never be built in any role; without the import name the head does not compile. Both are the smallest change that keeps each ticket's behaviour. |

## M1-30 Expiry of grants and memberships

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-30/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave7` at `8e30539`, branch `feat/M1-30-authz-expiry` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-30/2 | 2026-10-12 | No migration: `00005_authz_groups_grants.sql` (M1-27) already has `expiry_reminded_at` and `expiry_reported_at` on both tables, both `(expires_at)` indexes and the trigger that sets either grant column once. Only new sqlc queries (`queries/expiry.sql`). | Scope 1 of the brief: add only what the merged migration lacks. |
| M1-30/3 | 2026-10-12 | The jobs are `app.ExpiryService` (`Sweep`, `Remind`) in `app/expiry.go`; their arguments, handlers and `Register` live in the new package `adapters/jobs`, which `internal/wire` calls from `NewJobRegistry`. The selections and marks are the separate port `app.ExpiryStore`, implemented by the PostgreSQL repository and also needed by the rights hook (`RightsHookDeps.Expiry`); the in-memory fake store does not implement it. | `app` may not import `internal/platform` (only adapters may); `sync/adapters/jobs` is the same pattern. A separate port keeps the existing `Store` and its fake unchanged. |
| M1-30/4 | 2026-10-12 | The reporting of an expiry (selection, marker, `grant.changed` or `group.changed`, the audit entry, the views before) runs in the rights begin hook right after `permission_version` is locked, with the time of the change; the events wait for phase 3.1, where the appender accepts them. A sweep batch passes its kind and limit through the context and its work phase only reads `RightsHook.Folded`. The deny entry of a violation in a sweep batch names the resource kind (`grant` or `group`) without id, because a batch has many rows. | One clock and one lock for the selection and both views; the appender refuses events in phase 1. |
| M1-30/5 | 2026-10-12 | Every rights change folds the due, unreported expiries in before anything else (M1-30/4), not only the sweep: they are reported with event, audit entry and marker by the first unit after the expiry, and its `access.changed` carries the action of that unit. The views treat rows not due yet (and in a sweep batch the rows of a later batch) as not expired (`domain.AssumeValid.Unreported`). The brief's `AddAffectedAssumingValid` is not built: the fold records the views before itself. Outside the sweep the fold is not bounded by `authz.expiry_sweep_batch_max`. | A view must be what the client was told before the change and what `authorize` decides after it. With the expiry left to the sweep, three red team rounds reproduced lost losses and a reported gain that `authorize` denies (a membership and its group grant expiring together, a change between an expiry and the sweep, a new grant to a group of an expired member). The sweep runs every `authz.expiry_sweep_interval`, so a change folds the rows of at most one interval unless the worker was down. |
| M1-30/6 | 2026-10-12 | An invariant violation in a batch is logged as ERROR `expiry batch rejected by an invariant` with `bothy.action` and `error.type` (`i1_violation` or `last_owner`); the agent ids are in the deny audit entry the rights hook records. | The log handlers drop every attribute outside the signals catalogue, which has no agent id; the audit entry is the durable record. |
| M1-30/7 | 2026-10-12 | The reminder excludes grants of deleted groups and memberships of deleted groups in the selection itself. | A row skipped after the selection would never be marked and would fill every batch again. |
| M1-30/8 | 2026-10-12 | `access.expiring` carries `target` (kind, id, level, capabilities) only for space and channel grants; it is absent for the instance and for hosts. | Brief Scope 4.3 and 07 section 9.4. The schema `AccessTargetKind` also knows `host` for `access.expiring`; host grants exist from M2-39, whose ticket decides whether a host reminder names its target. |
| M1-30/9 | 2026-10-12 | `group.changed` carries the group with `member_count` = the memberships in effect at the time of the change, counted in phase 3.1 after the use case wrote its own memberships. | `Group.member_count` is required; counting at commit keeps it right when a change that adds or removes members also folds an expiry of the same group. |
| M1-30/10 | 2026-10-12 | `internal/wire` builds the service with the system clock. | A sweep batch uses the clock of the rights hook (M1-30/4); the reminder reads its own once per batch. |
| M1-30/11 | 2026-10-12 | `authz.Register` now declares `grant.changed`, `group.changed` and `access.expiring` next to `access.changed`. | authz appends them from this ticket on; the registration check rejects a second module for them. |
| M1-30/12 | 2026-10-12 | Configuration: `authz.expiry_sweep_interval` (duration, default `1m`, at least `1s`), `authz.expiry_reminder_lead` (duration, `48h`, at least `1s`) and `authz.expiry_sweep_batch_max` (int, `500`, 1 to 2147483647, the range of the SQL limit parameter). | 07 section 14 gives the defaults; the bounds keep every value usable. |
| M1-30/13 | 2026-10-12 | Recipients of `access.expiring` follow 07 section 9.4 and the brief: for a grant to a group all active owners and `granted_by`. The note of `access.expiring` in 05 section 6 (generated from `actions.yaml`: "an das Subjekt (bei Gruppen die Person)") is not changed here. | The brief rules this recipient set; the catalogue note is outside the files this ticket may change and goes to the specification lane. |
| M1-30/14 | 2026-10-12 | Both jobs are registered in the one job registry every role builds (as `sync.partition_maintenance` and `sync.idempotency_cleanup`); only `worker` and `all` run handlers, because only they start River (M1-14). The registration tests check queue, period and arguments, not the role. | An `api` process must accept exactly the kinds a worker runs; the role split is the job runtime's and is tested there. |
| M1-30/15 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (NEIN, one reproduced major): fixed through `AssumeValid.Unreported` (M1-30/5) with `TestExpirySweep_MembershipAndGroupGrantTogether` and `TestExpirySweep_TwoGrantsOnOneTargetInTwoBatches`. Minors fixed: the host target follows 07 (M1-30/8), the active owners are read once per reminder batch, and adapter tests cover the membership selections and marks. Minors kept: the invariant log without agent ids (M1-30/6); `grant.changed` of a channel target carries no `space_id` while `internal/wire` passes the empty structure source (until M1-53, as for the rights hook of M1-29). | Findings of the red team fixed in the same ticket where they are in scope. |
| M1-30/16 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 2 (NEIN, one reproduced major of the same class: a revocation or removal between an expiry and the sweep reported nothing, and a new grant in that window reported an `added` the client already had): fixed in one place, the views of the rights hook (M1-30/5), with the regression tests `TestExpiryWindow_*`. | The second finding of the same class: fixed for the class, not the instance. |
| M1-30/17 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 3 (NEIN, one reproduced major: a new grant to a group whose membership had expired unreported told the expired member `added` for a target `authorize` denies): fixed for the class by folding due expiries into every rights change (M1-30/4, /5), with `TestExpiryWindow_NoPhantomGain`, `TestExpiryWindow_GroupDeleteAndReAdd` and the changed `TestExpiryWindow_NewGrantAfterExpiry`. `TestExpirySweep_ExactlyOnce` makes its failing run with a vetoing subscriber of `authorize.AccessChanged`, because the sweep's events now go through the appender of the rights hook. | Third finding in the same area: the mechanism now has one rule (report a due expiry before computing any view) instead of assumptions in the views. |
| M1-30/18 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 4 (NEIN, one reproduced major: an expiry folded into a person's rights change was audited with that person as actor, with device, session and IP pseudonym): the audit entries of folded expiries are written from a context without the request principal and without the client IP (`systemContext`, the values are hidden by their types because the keys of `kernel/actor` and `kernel/reqmeta` are unexported); the correlation id of the change stays, so the entry points to the request that folded it. Test `TestExpiryWindow_FoldAuditIsSystem`. Minor fixed: `member_count` (M1-30/9). Minor kept: a denied admin request folds before its use case authorizes and then rolls the fold back; correct, only work. | System actions never pretend a human did them (05 rule 1.10, R-AZ-15); the brief asks the audit row without actor. |

## M1-31 People, owners and own profile

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-31/1 | 2026-10-12 | Built on `feat/M1-31-identity-people` from `int/m1-wave7` (`8e30539`) in its own worktree; not merged locally, not pushed; the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's local merge and `git worktree remove`. |
| M1-31/2 | 2026-10-12 | Resolves M1-20/4: the Idempotency-Key stage opens its unit with `txn.AllowLateRightsChange()`. The first nested `Do` that declares a rights change in such a unit runs the rights begin hook, its function and the rights check hook in this order inside the ambient transaction (`txn.UnitControl.Declare`, `platform/pg` `lateRightsChange`); sync and audit run at the end of the outer unit; a second rights change in the same unit is an internal error. | The stage has written only its key row when the use case starts, so `permission_version` is still locked before any domain row (03 "Sperrreihenfolge"), and only the use case knows the affected principals. Running the check at the end of the nested unit hands `i1_violation` and `last_owner` to the handler, so the stage stores them like any final `409`. |
| M1-31/3 | 2026-10-12 | The actor-based use cases are the ports `identity.People` and `identity.Owners` with the DTOs of the brief (`UserView` and `OwnerView` serve as user and owner entry); `identity.Directory` stays the actor-free read port of other modules. | The brief adds the methods to `Directory`, whose contract (M1-26) is "does not call authorize"; two meanings in one interface would trap the modules that use it. Only the names differ. |
| M1-31/4 | 2026-10-12 | `listUsers` and `listOwners` authorize `user.read` on the resource kind `user` without id and with `OwnerID` = the caller, not on the instance. | The catalogue gives `user.read` only the kind `user` (`authorize` fails closed with an internal error for `instance`). The descriptor owned by the caller is visible to every caller (M1-28/D4), so the requirement "human; Apps mit Scope" decides: people and apps read the directory, an agent gets `403 forbidden` (`subject_kind_not_allowed`) as test 11 asks. |
| M1-31/5 | 2026-10-12 | An owner with a personal token gets `403 forbidden` with `reason: owner_only` for the five owner writes, not `step_up_not_possible` as the brief's test 2 states. | Same as M1-29/18: the requirement step decides before the step-up step, and with a token the owner role does not take effect (SR5). The rights logic stays in authz. |
| M1-31/6 | 2026-10-12 | `updateMyProfile` by an agent or app is `403 forbidden` with `reason: subject_kind_not_allowed`, decided by identity after `authorize` allowed `self.profile.update`. | The requirement of `self.profile.update` is `self`, which an agent naming itself meets; the profile is a row of `users`, which only people have (03 users), so the use case refuses the kind like a missing resource of the wrong kind, with the reason code of 07 section 4.1. |
| M1-31/7 | 2026-10-12 | `createUser` emits `user.updated`; `addOwner` for a person that is not active is `409 conflict` (`reason: principal_not_active`), for an owner `409 conflict` (`reason: already_owner`); `enableUser` on an active person is an audited call without effect (`200`, `no_change: true`, no event, no rights version); `revokeOwner` without an active owners row is `404`. | Rulings of the brief; the reasons name the cause (06 section 5 point 3). |
| M1-31/8 | 2026-10-12 | `local_account: true` on `createUser` is `400 validation_failed` with pointer `/local_account` and code `not_enabled` until M1-37 adds enrollment codes; M1-37 replaces this rule. | Interim rule of the brief; `not_enabled` is the field code of 06 section 5 point 3 for a function this server version does not build yet. |
| M1-31/9 | 2026-10-12 | `addOwner`, `revokeOwner` and `enableUser` authorize once per request in a first unit without rights change (also loading the resource and checking reason and state), then write in a second unit declared as the rights change, which locks the person (the owner changes also the active owners rows, in ascending id) and checks the state again but not the authorization. Every rights change is serialized by the lock on `permission_version` of the rights begin hook, so I1 and SR8 hold for `enableUser` without the owners lock. Two owners revoking each other at once give one `204` and one `409 last_owner`. A raced `enableUser` that finds the person already active answers as without effect (no event); inside the Idempotency-Key stage the rights version of that attempt may have been raised. | A request is judged by the rights at its start (R-RE-02, one rights version per request); SR8 and I1 guard the result under the lock (test 5 of the brief). A refused call never raises the rights version. |
| M1-31/10 | 2026-10-12 | `q` of `listUsers` uses `starts_with(lower(column), lower(q))` instead of `LIKE` with escaped `%` and `_`. | The same literal prefix match without an escape rule to get wrong; held by test 11. |
| M1-31/11 | 2026-10-12 | Locales are parsed by `adapters/locale` (`golang.org/x/text/language`, vendor group `xtext`, now a direct dependency of `go.mod`) behind the port `app.Locales`; the domain imports `time/tzdata` for `time.LoadLocation` on hosts without zoneinfo. A time zone must also have the canonical form of an IANA name (`domain.zonePattern`), because the server image does carry a system zoneinfo directory whose other files (`posixrules`, `right/...`, `posix/...`) and spellings (`Europe//Berlin`, `./UTC`, lower case on a case-insensitive file system) `time.LoadLocation` accepts too. | The module register grants vendors to adapters only (go-arch-lint `depOnAnyVendor: false`); x/text is BSD-3-Clause and was already in `go.sum`. Red team round 1, Major 4. |
| M1-31/12 | 2026-10-12 | `getMe` reads the rights version through `instance.Versions`, pins it with `authorize.WithPermissionVersion` and then authorizes `self.read`, so `permission_version` is exactly the version of the check. | The version of the Authorizer's request memo is private to authz; pinning needs no new port. |
| M1-31/13 | 2026-10-12 | Responses are validated by `httpservertest.ResponseValidator` over `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator` (already the request validator's library, JSON Schema 2020-12 through `santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` inside it) and the harness option `ValidateResponses`, not by a new `test/apitest` package with a direct `santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema` dependency. The test authenticator `httpservertest.Actors` with `InSession` and `WithToken` builds the principals of the tests. | The brief allows "the response validator of the merged tree"; reusing the library of the request side adds no dependency and no component outside the module register. |
| M1-31/14 | 2026-10-12 | A handle with upper case is `400` (`pattern`) before uniqueness is checked, so "duplicate handle in other case" of test 8 is a `400`; a duplicate handle in the same case and a duplicate e-mail in any case are `409 conflict` (`/handle`, `/email`). | The check and the API pattern allow lower case only (M1-26); the unique index on `lower(email)` covers e-mail. |
| M1-31/15 | 2026-10-12 | The schema `User` has no e-mail member, so `getUser`, `listUsers`, the owner list and `user.updated` never carry an e-mail address; only `getMe` reports the caller's own. `audit.reason_max_chars` (19 SEC-AUD-5) is not enforced on the reasons of `enableUser`, `addOwner` and `revokeOwner`: the key is not registered yet and the brief adds no key; the body limit of the chain bounds them. | Contract of `docs/api/openapi.yaml`; the length rule belongs to the ticket that registers the key. |
| M1-31/16 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`, four Majors), fixed with a test each: (1) `GetUser`, `UpdateUser`, `EnableUser`, `AddOwner`, `RevokeOwner` and the device reads `getDevice` and `listUserDevices` of M1-33 call `authorize` before they answer a missing person, owner role or device with `404`, so an anonymous caller, a pending device or a person without owner role gets the same answer for an existing and an unknown id (`checkThenFound`; the second occurrence of the class, fixed for both tickets); (2) `q` of `listUsers` must be valid UTF-8 without control or bidi characters (`400`, `#query/q`), a cursor whose handle is no valid handle is `cursor_invalid`, and SQLSTATE `22021` maps to `400`; (3) a locale longer than 35 characters (RFC 5646 section 4.4.1) is `400 too_long` before it is parsed; (4) the strict zone form of M1-31/11. Minors fixed: a failed late rights change fails the whole unit even when its caller swallows the error, and a nested unit the late declaration covers may join while it runs; an empty `locale` or `time_zone` on `createUser` is `400` like on `updateUser` (`CreateUserCmd` takes pointers); the privacy test captures the access log of `slog.Default`; the response validator test has the undeclared status. Not changed: REST times are truncated to milliseconds but encoded by `encoding/json` without trailing zeros (also in M1-33); 06 section 2 lets clients read every RFC 3339 form, and the encoding belongs to the generated layer of all operations, not to this ticket. | Rules of 07 section 4.2 and 02 section 9.2 (no existence oracle), SEC-T06 (no unexpected 5xx), SEC-IN-4. |
| M1-31/17 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 2 (`NEIN`: two Blockers, two Majors), fixed with a test each: (B1) the handler tests build one response validator per process, contract and operation list, over the contract pruned to the mounted operations like the request validator (`httpservertest.SharedResponseValidator`, validation serialized; one build under `-race` went from about 72 s to about 6 s and the package from 275 s to 55 s in this run), so the HTTP test package of identity stays far below the 10 minute limit of `go test -race`; (B2) the use cases, the people queries, `httpservertest` and `kernel/txn.UnitControl` have tests in their own packages (`identity/app/people_test.go`, `adapters/postgres/people_test.go`, `httpservertest_test.go`, `txn/late_test.go`), so the coverage of new code does not depend on tests of other packages; (M1) a cursor handle is checked against the handle pattern only, not `principal.handle_max_chars`, so a handle created under a larger limit still pages; (M2) a time zone must be one of the IANA names of the toolchain's tzdata, generated into `domain/zones.gen.go` by `tools/zonegen` (without `Factory`, which ICU refuses), which makes the rule the same on case-insensitive and case-sensitive hosts. Minors fixed: `UpdateUser` authorizes before it locks the row; a recovered panic in a late rights change fails the unit; the locale bound counts characters and its value is pinned by a test; the docs of the device reads, the owners lock of `enableUser` and the `CheckSearchPrefix` comment. M1-33/2 ("an unknown person is not_found before authorize") is superseded by M1-31/16 for `listUserDevices`. Not changed: a raced `enableUser` and the swallowed `errRaced` of authz (M1-48 must hand it on behind the stage, noted at `txn.AllowLateRightsChange`); the reason length of SEC-AUD-5 (M1-31/15); unchecked rights writes after a late rights change in the same unit (no caller; the rule is in the godoc). | Same rules as round 1; the test-time limit and Sonar's new-code coverage are gates of CI. |
| M1-31/18 | 2026-10-13 | The test binary of `internal/wire` builds the request validator of the enabled operations in `TestMain` (`WarmEnabledValidator`, `export_test.go`) before any test starts. | Second occurrence of the class of M1-33/18: with thirteen enabled operations the one build per process grew, and under `-race` and a host load around 25 the first serve tests (`TestServeTLSPublicListener`, `TestConsumerLagGaugesInApiRole`, `TestStart_*`) waited on it inside their 30 s readiness wait and timed out in `make check`, while they pass alone. Building it before the tests removes the build from every per-test deadline, whatever later tickets enable. |

## M1-32 Signing keys, JWKS and rotation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-32/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave7` at `8e30539`, branch `feat/M1-32-signing-keys` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-32/2 | 2026-10-12 | `audit_checkpoint` is a valid purpose of `rotateSigningKey` (`200`), unlike the brief's test 7; `terminal` is refused with `400 validation_failed` (`/purpose`, `not_supported`) until M2-39 manages it. | `SigningKeyPurpose` in openapi.yaml lists `audit_checkpoint` and `terminal`, and 19 section 10 rotates `audit_checkpoint` keys only on owner request through `signing_key.rotate`; without it a leaked checkpoint key (Fall D, "alle Zwecke rotieren") could never be replaced. The brief's premise that the schema lacks the purpose is wrong for the specification it cites. |
| M1-32/3 | 2026-10-12 | An owner with a personal token gets `403 forbidden` with `reason: owner_only`, not `step_up_not_possible` as the brief's test 7 says. | 07 section 4.2 checks the requirement (step 6) before step-up (step 8), and a personal token never carries the owner role (SR5, `authz.PrincipalFacts.IsOwner`), so `authz` (M1-28) refuses at step 6. Both answers are `403`; changing the order of `authz` is outside this ticket. |
| M1-32/4 | 2026-10-12 | Audit details carry a `kid` (`new_kid`, `previous_kid`, `kid`) as the hex form of the thumbprint, 64 lower-case characters (`app.KIDCode`). | A `kid` is base64url with upper-case letters, which `audit_details.v1` refuses in a code (SEC-AUD-5: `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.:-]*$`); hex is the same SHA-256 value and converts back without loss, and the schema needs no new value kind. |
| M1-32/5 | 2026-10-12 | `Rotate`, the job and `EnsureActive` run their key transaction in a unit of work of their own (`txn.Detach`), also inside the Idempotency-Key stage. | Private files of retired keys are deleted after the commit; inside the stage's transaction a later rollback would restore rows whose files are gone. A repeat after a failed store of the response repeats the rotation: `scheduled` returns the same `next` key, `emergency` creates one more key, both harmless. |
| M1-32/6 | 2026-10-12 | `getJwks` authorizes `instance.discovery.read` for the principal of the request (anonymous allowed) in `Keys.JWKS`. | The proof of the check (02 "Nachweis der Prüfung") turns any successful response without a check of the route's action into `500 authz_not_checked`. |
| M1-32/7 | 2026-10-12 | `EnsureActive` retires every unretired key whose private file is missing and creates a fresh active key when none is left (`domain.LostTransitions`), logging `signing key file missing, key retired` at level error. | After a restore without the key store (Fall B, an old bundle) the restored rows name files that do not exist: without the repair no access token could be signed, so no owner could sign in to rotate. 16 section 5.4 already names the consequence of lost signing keys (tokens invalid, devices sign in again). Every role must mount the same key store; the README says so. |
| M1-32/8 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy secrets export` adds the key store under `signing-keys/` with the flag `--signing-key-dir` (default `/var/lib/bothy/signing-keys`, skipped with a note on stderr when the default does not exist); `secrets init` skips `signing-keys/` in its source. | The command reads no configuration, so the default of `secrets.signing_key_dir` is repeated; Compose mounts the key store read-only in `secrets-init` at that path. A decrypted bundle is the source of `init` in Fall B, which would otherwise refuse the key files as unknown. |
| M1-32/9 | 2026-10-12 | The private key of a loaded key sits in a `secrets.Value` (the seed); the expanded key exists only during `Sign`. `Signer` checks that the loaded key belongs to the public key of the row. | SEC-SEC-3 and the redacting type of M1-09; a swapped file must not sign under another `kid`. |
| M1-32/10 | 2026-10-12 | New telemetry attributes `bothy.signing_key.purpose`, `bothy.signing_key.kid`, `bothy.signing_key.from_state`, `bothy.signing_key.to_state` (logs of key transitions only). | The log handlers drop every key outside the catalog; the brief asks for one line per transition with purpose, `kid` and both states. |
| M1-32/11 | 2026-10-12 | `Provider.Put` and `Provider.Delete` return `error` only and take a parsed `secrets.Ref` and a `secrets.Value`; the file driver implements `Writer` (with `Delete`). Writes are allowed only directly in `secrets.signing_key_dir`, not in subdirectories. | The names of M1-09 win over the brief's sketch (`ref string`, `Secret`); the key files never need a subdirectory, so the narrower rule costs nothing. |
| M1-32/12 | 2026-10-12 | The migration is `00010_server_signing_keys.sql` with the table exactly as 03, including the purpose `terminal` in its check and the EdDSA check for `invocation`, `terminal` and `webhook_out`. | Next number on the base; PLAN row "Nachtrag vom 2026-10-10" asks M1-32 to create the purpose `terminal` with its table without using it. |
| M1-32/13 | 2026-10-12 | When the plan of a key change, recomputed under the advisory lock, asks for a key that was not generated before (another process changed the keys between the read and the lock), the transaction rolls back, a key is generated and the change runs again, at most three times, then `409 conflict`. | Red team round 1, major 1: `EnsureActive` could leave a purpose without an active key and still succeed (`TestEnsureActive_LostNextPromotedConcurrently`). |
| M1-32/14 | 2026-10-12 | The readiness check `signing_keys` also fails for a purpose whose active key cannot sign (unreadable private file, or a file of another key), through `Keys.Unready`. | Red team round 1, minor: a key store with unreadable files (a changed `secrets.signing_key_dir`, a role without the volume) looked ready while `Signer` failed. |

## Integration int/m1-wave8

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W8/1 | 2026-10-13 | M1-30, M1-31 and M1-32 enter as one squash each onto `8e30539` (`int/m1-wave7`), in this order, each with only its own commits (`8e30539..<head>`). Append-only files (DECISIONS, the server CHANGELOG, `api/operations.yaml`) are unions; `operations.yaml` enables the operations of both M1-31 and M1-32 in ascending order and drops `getJwks` and `getMe` from `later`, because each ticket had removed only its own entry. `api/rest/rest.gen.go` and `api/routes/routes.gen.go` are regenerated by `go generate` from the merged list; sqlc, the arch lint config, `config-schema.json`, the configuration reference and the catalogue regenerate without a diff. `moduleRegistrations` lists `identity.Register` and `authz.Register` (M1-31 order). `wire.Handlers` embeds the people handlers of M1-31 and the key handlers of M1-32; `startJobs` registers the expiry jobs of M1-30 and the key rotation of M1-32. | Rule of the integration branch: union for append-only files, generators for generated files, no change of ticket behaviour. |
| INT-W8/2 | 2026-10-13 | `wire.NewJobRegistry` refuses a zero interval of each job since M1-30 (`authz.expiry_sweep_interval`) and M1-32 (`signing_key.rotation_check_interval`); the wire tests that build a registry now set both intervals, and the negative test of M1-32 sets the expiry interval, so it still fails only on the missing rotation interval. The identity HTTP tests of M1-31 used a local strict server with the device and people handlers, which no longer implements the strict interface once M1-32 adds `getJwks` and `rotateSigningKey`; they now build the routes over `wire.Handlers`, the production composition. | Semantic conflicts that only the merged tree shows (`TestKeyRotationJobRegistered` and `TestAuthzExpiryJobsRegistered` failed, the identity test package did not compile); test-side fixes, no production behaviour changes. |

## M1-34 Sessions, access tokens, refresh families and logout

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-34/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave8` at `f6c34de`, branch `feat/M1-34-identity-sessions` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-34/2 | 2026-10-13 | Session, device and key state are read per request, not cached: the Authenticator reads session, device and the device's active `dpop` key in one indexed query and reports `SessionActive`, `DeviceState` and `DeviceKeyActive` in the actor; `authz` takes these facts through the optional port `authorize.CredentialObserver` in `check_active` instead of the facts it cached for the rights version. A principal without observed facts (API tokens until M1-44, test principals) keeps the credential source. Logout and refresh reuse do not raise `permission_version`. 04 section 8.5 ("Cache, der an der Rechte-Version hängt") holds for grants; for sessions and devices of people this ruling applies. | The brief's ruling: revocation then works across processes at once (AZ-P9 for sessions, `TestAuthenticate_RevocationAcrossInstances`) without a rights change for every logout; the cost is one indexed query per authenticated request. A session never comes back, so the cached flag could only be stale in the allowing direction. |
| M1-34/3 | 2026-10-13 | `listUserSessions` authorizes `session.list` on the resource `user` (the catalogue's only resource kind of the action) and answers the decision `forbidden` with reason `not_self` for another person with `404 not_found`; an unknown person is `404` after `authorize` as well. | 05 rule 2: the sessions of another person are not readable for the caller, so the answer is `404`, as the brief asks. With the kind `user` authz sees a visible person (the directory) and refuses at the requirement; the catalogue lists no owned kind for `session.list` (unlike `device.list`, M1-33/2), and changing 05 is a core contract change outside this ticket. |
| M1-34/4 | 2026-10-13 | A refresh of a person in status `pending` succeeds like one from a pending device; `disabled` and `deleted` are `401 session_revoked`. | The brief says "principal not active", but 08 R-ID-14 gives provisioned people tokens with the rights of a pending device, and 05 section 2.5 lets them call `auth.token.refresh`; refusing would log them out before the owner approves them. |
| M1-34/5 | 2026-10-13 | A missing required `DPoP` header is answered by the request validator of `platform/httpserver` with `401 invalid_dpop_proof` and the challenge `DPoP error="invalid_dpop_proof", algs="ES256 EdDSA"`, without `DPoP-Nonce`. | The validator stage runs before the handler, so the generated decoder never sees the request; the chain has no nonce source, and the next proof failure answers with the nonce. The rule holds for every operation with the parameter (refresh and the login operations). |
| M1-34/6 | 2026-10-13 | The chain passes every value of the `DPoP` header (`actor.Credentials.DPoPProofs`), puts the DPoP facts of the request (`reqmeta.Proof`: method, expected htu, values) and a setter of response headers (`reqmeta.SetResponseHeader`) into the context. The Authenticator keeps the signature of M1-18 (`Authenticate(ctx, actor.Credentials)`), not the brief's `Authenticate(r)`. | Two DPoP headers must fail (RFC 9449 section 4.3), the refresh handler needs method and htu without the request, and `DPoP-Nonce` after a stale nonce has to reach the response from code that has no response writer. |
| M1-34/7 | 2026-10-13 | The session part of identity starts in its own step `sessions` right after `signing_keys` (`Identity.BindSessions`); identity stays right after authz (INT-W7/3). Until then the chain would authenticate with `actor.AnonymousOnly`. | Access tokens are signed with the keys of instance, which exist only after `signing_keys`; the routes need the Authenticator. |
| M1-34/8 | 2026-10-13 | The port `identity.Auth` has one method beyond the brief's list: `CreateExtensionSession(ctx, actor, component)`, the use case of `createExtensionSession` (authorize `session.extension.create`, `StartChildSession`, audit). A replaced child ends with `logout`, without actor in its `session.revoked`. | `StartChildSession` takes no actor (the brief's signature), and the handler must stay free of authorization and audit (adapters only convert). |
| M1-34/9 | 2026-10-13 | Refresh denials carry the reasons of the brief and the cause codes `unknown_token`, `revoked_state`, `refresh_expired`, `dpop_key_mismatch` and `refresh_reuse` (with `family_id`); an expired refresh token is audited too. Reuse writes its deny entry inside the unit of work that commits the revocation; every other denial goes to `RecordDenial` after the rollback. | SEC-AUD-5 allows codes only; a cause per denial makes the audit log readable without tokens or hashes. |
| M1-34/10 | 2026-10-13 | The migration `00011_identity_sessions.sql` creates all four table checks of 03 `auth_sessions` (the brief counts three; 03 also lists `step_up_consumed_at IS NULL OR step_up_at IS NOT NULL`), defers the foreign key `identity_id -> user_identities(id)` to M1-36, and grants `bothy_app` `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on the three tables, no `DELETE`. | 03 is normative; rows end by `revoked_at` and `used_at` and are security history, deleted only by the cleanup and retention jobs, which grant their delete. |
| M1-34/11 | 2026-10-13 | Activity is written by one statement (`TouchSession`, a CTE over `auth_sessions` and `devices`) on the pool, outside the request's unit of work, each row only when its `last_seen_at` is unset or older than `identity.last_active_granularity`; a failure is logged at warn level with the error kind and the correlation id. | Brief "Authenticator" item 8; the guard in the statement also holds when two processes see the same old value. |
| M1-34/12 | 2026-10-13 | `kernel/jose` gets `SignerFunc`; the access token issuer wraps `instance.Signer.Sign(ctx, ...)` in it. | The deep scan of go-arch-lint refuses a type of `identity/app` handed into `kernel/jose` (a kernel component calling a method of a module); a function value of a kernel type keeps the direction. |
| M1-34/13 | 2026-10-13 | Lint exclusions: gosec G117 for `api/rest/rest.gen.go` (the contract's `TokenResponse` marshals `access_token`) and G101 for `identity/adapters/postgres/sqlcgen/refresh.sql.go` (query names containing "token"). | Generated code that must look like this; the tokens leave the server only through `TokenResponse`, and the queries bind HMACs only. |
| M1-34/14 | 2026-10-13 | Access tokens are verified with the algorithm allowlist `EdDSA` (the only algorithm of the server keys, ADR 0066); a key in state `next` is refused like an unknown one (`instance.Keys.VerificationKey` also returns `next` keys). The Bearer branch answers `invalid_dpop_proof` when the token parses with header `typ` `at+jwt` and names a key of purpose `access_token` in state `active` or `retiring`, without a signature check. | 08 section 7.2 (`kid` among `active` or `retiring`); the Bearer answer is `401` either way, so a forged token learns nothing. |
| M1-34/15 | 2026-10-13 | ID-T27 for `math/rand`: the depguard rule `crypto-rand-only-in-identity` in `services/server/.golangci.yml`, held by `test_identity_bans_math_rand` (tools/ci) and proven once on a probe file in a temporary copy of the module (golangci-lint reported `import 'math/rand/v2' is not allowed`). | The brief allows a golangci fixture or a probe in a temporary copy; a test that runs golangci-lint inside `go test` would need the pinned binary in every test environment. |
| M1-34/16 | 2026-10-13 | Not built: the refresh without rotation in maintenance mode (08 section 7.4, ADR 0065). | `server.maintenance_mode` and the stage `StageMaintenance` arrive with M7-08; the brief does not list it. |
| M1-34/17 | 2026-10-13 | `refreshToken` is an operation without security (`security: []`): a client sends no `Authorization` header with it, only the `DPoP` proof. A request that carries one goes through the Authenticator like any other (an expired access token is `401 token_expired`, a valid one consumes the proof's `jti` before the handler). | RFC 9449 section 5: the refresh request proves the key with the proof alone; the contract declares the operation public. |
| M1-34/18 | 2026-10-13 | Red team round 1 (`JA`), minors fixed with a test each: the session tree is locked in two statements, the session row first and then its children, so a child inserted by the holder of the parent lock is revoked with the parent (`TestRevokeParent_ChildCreatedConcurrently`); a refresh whose family key is no longer the device's active `dpop` key is `401 session_revoked` (`TestRefresh_DeviceKeyRevoked`); an activity write without a valid client IP keeps the stored `last_ip` (`TestTouch_KeepsLastIP`). Round 2 (`JA`): the concurrency test now waits until the logout waits on the lock before the child commits. | One READ COMMITTED statement re-checks only the rows it found after waiting for a lock, so it missed the new child; a refresh would otherwise issue tokens `check_active` refuses. |

## M1-35 Step-up with the device key

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-35/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave9` at `668ef20`, branch `feat/M1-35-identity-step-up` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language (as M1-34/1). |
| M1-35/2 | 2026-10-13 | `StepUp` is a port of its own in `internal/identity/stepup.go`, beside `Auth`; 02 section 4.3 lists step-up under `Auth`. | The brief fixes the names and the file; M1-40 adds `CompleteWithPasskey` to the same port. The README's port list names both. |
| M1-35/3 | 2026-10-13 | The migration `00012_identity_step_up.sql` creates `auth_challenges` with every column, check, unique and index of 03 but defers the foreign key `enrollment_code_id -> enrollment_codes(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`: the migration that creates `enrollment_codes` (M1-37) adds it. `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` (the cleanup job deletes). | `enrollment_codes` does not exist on the base; the column and its pairing check exist, so only the constraint is missing (as M1-34/10 for `identity_id`). |
| M1-35/4 | 2026-10-13 | Answers: a wrong confirmation is `401 step_up_required` (the one that reaches `auth.step_up_fail_limit` is `401 session_revoked`); an unknown, foreign, other purpose, finished or expired challenge is `401 step_up_required` and not counted; a caller who is no person, has no session, sits on a `cli` device or whose device has no active `step_up` key gets `403 forbidden` with reason `step_up_not_possible`. Written into 08 section 6.2. | Brief "Choice of codes": the client's correct reaction to a failed confirmation is a new step-up (06 section 5), not a refresh or a login; where a step-up is impossible a retry cannot help (08 section 13). |
| M1-35/5 | 2026-10-13 | The anonymous principal is not refused with `403 step_up_not_possible` by the actor check; it goes to `authorize`, which answers `401 unauthenticated`. | A request without credentials must be told to authenticate (06 section 3); the actor check is for principals that are authenticated but cannot give a step-up. |
| M1-35/6 | 2026-10-13 | The deny entry of a counted failure is recorded inside the unit of work that commits the failed challenge and the revocation (`Record`), not after the commit; denials that roll back go to `RecordDenial` afterwards. Details: `{cause, challenge_id, session_revoked}`, never the value or the signature. | The entry then exists exactly when the failure does (as the refresh reuse of M1-34/9); the brief's order "commit, then audit" would lose the entry when the process ends between the two. |
| M1-35/7 | 2026-10-13 | Every confirmation that fails the check counts, also one that is not a JWS of the profile (`invalid_jws`, which includes `crit`, `jku`, `x5u`, `x5c`, `zip` refused by `kernel/jose`), one with a `jwk` header (`forbidden_header`) and one with another `alg` (`alg_mismatch`). | The caller presented a valid open challenge of its own session, so it is a confirmation attempt; counting only good-looking JWS would give an attacker unlimited free attempts. |
| M1-35/8 | 2026-10-13 | `iat` is compared in whole seconds: valid is `iat >= floor(created_at - auth.clock_leeway)` and `iat <= now + auth.clock_leeway`. | `iat` is an integer NumericDate; without the cut a client that signs in the same second as the issue fails with `auth.clock_leeway = 0`. |
| M1-35/9 | 2026-10-13 | The job `identity.auth_cleanup` deletes a challenge after `expires_at`, but a failed step-up challenge only once its `finished_at` has left `auth.login_fail_window`; 08 section 13 and the retention row of 20 say so. | The failures are counted from these rows; deleting them after `auth.step_up_challenge_ttl` (2m) would reset the count inside the 15m window. 20 said "expired rows"; the implementation proved that incomplete (spec first, brief Scope 9). The job row of 02 stays a summary that points to 08. |
| M1-35/10 | 2026-10-13 | The active `step_up` key is read inside the unit of work after the session rows are locked, before the challenge; the answer for a device without key is the same as the brief's step 2. | A key revoked between the read and the lock cannot then confirm; the order of the answers stays the brief's (key before challenge). |
| M1-35/11 | 2026-10-13 | Both operations audit their allow (`step: challenge` and `step: confirm` with `method`); a successful step-up does not reset the failure count. Times are cut to microseconds, so the answer equals the stored `step_up_at`. | 05 marks `auth.step_up` "Audit ja" for the action, not one operation; 08 is silent on a reset, and a reset would let an attacker with the device interleave successes to keep guessing. |
| M1-35/12 | 2026-10-13 | The integration tests run in the session world of M1-34 (`sessions_env_test.go`, now with the real authorizer and its fake structure source in reach). AZ-M36 builds the Marketing space and channel in that fake structure source instead of reusing the matrix world of `authz/app`, which lives in another test package. | Test packages cannot import each other; principal, session and step-up stay real as the brief asks. |
